\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]

- Point \(A (6, 4)\): \(x_1 = 6\) and \(y_1 = 4\)
- Point \(B (-2, 8)\): \(x_2 = -2\) and \(y_2 = 8\)

- \(\Delta y = y_2 - y_1 = 8 - 4 = 4\)
- \(\Delta x = x_2 - x_1 = -2 - 6 = -8\)

Next, I recognize that 120 degrees is in the second quadrant where tangent values are negative. To simplify, I express 120 degrees as \( 180^\circ - 60^\circ \), which means \( \tan(120^\circ) = \tan(180^\circ - 60^\circ) \).

Using the identity for tangent of a difference, I have \( \tan(180^\circ - \theta) = -\tan(\theta) \). Therefore, \( \tan(120^\circ) = -\tan(60^\circ) \).

Since \( \tan(60^\circ) = \sqrt{3} \), substituting back gives me \( m = -\sqrt{3} \).

Thus, the slope of the line is \( -\sqrt{3} \).

To determine the slope of a line that makes an angle of **120°** with the positive direction of the x-axis, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Relationship Between Slope and Angle
The slope (\( m \)) of a straight line is related to the angle (\( \theta \)) it makes with the positive direction of the x-axis by the formula:
\[ m = \tan(\theta) \]

### Step 2: Substitute the Given Angle into the Formula
Given \( \theta = 120^\circ \), substitute this value into the formula:
\[ m = \tan(120^\circ) \]

### Step 3: Calculate the Tangent of 120°
Recognize that \( 120^\circ \) is in the second quadrant where tangent values are negative. Express \( 120^\circ \) as:
\[ 120^\circ = 180^\circ - 60^\circ \]
Using the identity for tangent of an angle supplementary to another angle:
\[ \tan(180^\circ - \theta) = -\tan(\theta) \]
So,
\[ \tan(120^\circ) = \tan(180^\circ - 60^\circ) = -\tan(60^\circ) \]

We know that:
\[ \tan(60^\circ) = \sqrt{3} \]
Therefore:
\[ \tan(120^\circ) = -\sqrt{3} \]

### Step 4: Determine the Slope
Substitute back into the slope formula:
\[ m = -\sqrt{3} \]

To find a point on the **x-axis** that is equidistant from the points \((9, 8)\) and \((5, 6)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Understand the Problem
A general point on the **x-axis** has coordinates of the form \((x, 0)\). We need to find such a point that is equidistant from both \((9, 8)\) and \((5, 6)\).

### Step 2: Use the Distance Formula
The distance between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
\[
\text{Distance} = \sqrt{(x_2 - x_1)^2 + (y_2 - y_1)^2}
\]

Since the distances from \((x, 0)\) to both \((9, 8)\) and \((5, 6)\) are equal, set up the equation:
\[
\sqrt{(9 - x)^2 + (8 - 0)^2} = \sqrt{(5 - x)^2 + (6 - 0)^2}
\]

### Step 3: Square Both Sides to Eliminate Square Roots
\[
(9 - x)^2 + 8^2 = (5 - x)^2 + 6^2
\]
\[
(81 - 18x + x^2) + 64 = (25 - 10x + x^2) + 36
\]

### Step 4: Simplify the Equation
Combine like terms:
\[
145 - 18x = 61 - 10x
\]
Subtract \(61\) from both sides and add \(18x\) to both sides:
\[
84 = 8x
\]

### Step 5: Solve for \(x\)
\[
x = \frac{84}{8} = 10.5
\]

### Final Answer

To determine if the points \( (4, 4) \), \( (3, 5) \), and \( (-1, -1) \) form a right-angled triangle without using the Pythagorean theorem, we can use vector analysis.

### Step-by-Step Explanation:

1. **Define Points:**
   Let's denote the points as:
   \[
   A(4, 4), \quad B(3, 5), \quad C(-1, -1)
   \]

2. **Calculate Vectors for Each Side:**
   
   - **Vector \( \vec{AB} \):** From point \( A \) to point \( B \):
     \[
     \vec{AB} = (3 - 4, 5 - 4) = (-1, 1)
     \]
     
   - **Vector \( \vec{BC} \):** From point \( B \) to point \( C \):
     \[
     \vec{BC} = (-1 - 3, -1 - 5) = (-4, -6)
     \]
     
   - **Vector \( \vec{CA} \):** From point \( C \) to point \( A \):
     \[
     \vec{CA} = (4 - (-1), 4 - (-1)) = (5, 5)
     \]

3. **Check for Perpendicular Vectors:**
   Two vectors are perpendicular if their dot product is zero.

   
   - **Dot Product of \( \vec{AB} \) and \( \vec{BC} \):**
     \[
     (-1)(-4) + (1)(-6) = 4 - 6 = -2 \neq 0
     \]
     
   - **Dot Product of \( \vec{BC} \) and \( \vec{CA} \):**
     \[
     (-4)(5) + (-6)(5) = -20 - 30 = -50 \neq 0
     \]
     
   - **Dot Product of \( \vec{CA} \) and \( \vec{AB} \):**
     \[
     (5)(-1) + (5)(1) = -5 + 5 = 0
     \]

4. **Conclusion:**
   The dot product of vectors \( \vec{CA} \) and \( \vec{AB} \) is zero, which means the angle between these two sides at point \( A \) is a right angle.

To determine if all four points \((-2, -1)\), \((4, 0)\), and \((3, 3)\) lie on the same straight line without using the distance formula, we can check their slopes.

### Step 1: Calculate the slope between two initial points
Let's consider the first two points:
- **Point A**: \((-2, -1)\)
- **Point B**: \((4, 0)\)

The slope \(m\) between Point A and Point B is calculated as:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{0 - (-1)}{4 - (-2)} = \frac{1}{6}
\]

### Step 2: Determine the equation of the line
Using the slope \(m = \frac{1}{6}\) and Point A, we can write the equation of the line in point-slope form:
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]
Substituting the values:
\[
y - (-1) = \frac{1}{6}(x - (-2))
\]
Simplifying:
\[
y + 1 = \frac{1}{6}(x + 2)
\]
Multiply both sides by 6 to eliminate the fraction:
\[
6(y + 1) = x + 2 \\
6y + 6 = x + 2 \\
x - 6y - 4 = 0
\]

### Step 3: Check if the third point lies on this line
Now, let's verify if **Point C** \((3, 3)\) satisfies the equation \(x - 6y - 4 = 0\):
\[
3 - 6(3) - 4 = 3 - 18 - 4 = -19 \neq 0
\]
Since the result is not zero, **Point C** does not lie on the line formed by Point A and Point B.

### Conclusion
All three points \((-2, -1)\), \((4, 0)\), and \((3, 3)\) do not lie on the same straight line because they do not satisfy the same linear equation when checked against each other.

The problem requires finding the slopes of two lines where one slope is twice the other and the tangent of the angle between them is 1/3. By setting up the equation using the formula for tanθ, we solve for m₁ and find multiple valid solutions.

**Step-by-step explanation:**

1. **Define Variables:**
   Let the slopes be \( m_1 \) (first line) and \( m_2 = 2m_1 \) (second line).

2. **Use Formula for Tangent of Angle Between Two Lines:**
   The formula is:
   \[
   \tan\theta = \left|\frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2}\right|
   \]
   
3. **Set Up the Equation with Given Information:**
   Substituting \( m_2 = 2m_1 \) and \( \tan\theta = \frac{1}{3} \):
   \[
   \left|\frac{2m_1 - m_1}{1 + m_1 (2m_1)}\right| = \frac{1}{3}
   \]
   
4. **Simplify the Equation:**
   Simplify numerator and denominator:
   \[
   \left|\frac{m_1}{1 + 2m_1^2}\right| = \frac{1}{3}
   \]
   
5. **Remove Absolute Value by Considering Both Cases:**
   Case 1: Positive  
   \( \frac{m_1}{1 + 2m_1^2} = \frac{1}{3} \)  
   Multiply both sides by denominator:
   \[
   3m_1 = 1 + 2m_1^2
   \]
   Rearrange to form quadratic equation:
   \[
   2m_1^2 - 3m_1 + 1 = 0
   \]
   
6. **Solve the Quadratic Equation:**
   Using quadratic formula \( m = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a} \):
   Here, a=2, b=-3, c=1:
   \[
   m_1 = \frac{3 \pm \sqrt{9 - 8}}{4} = \frac{3 \pm 1}{4}
   \]
   So,
   \( m_1 = 1 \) or \( m_1 = 0.5 \).

7. **Find Corresponding \( m_2 \) Values:**
   If \( m_1 = 1 \), then \( m_2 = 2(1) = 2 \).  
   If \( m_1 = 0.5 \), then \( m_2 = 2(0.5) = 1 \).

8. **Consider the Negative Case (Case 2):**
   \( \frac{m_1}{1 + 2m_1^2} = -\frac{1}{3} \)  
   Multiply both sides:
   \[
   3m_1 = -1 - 2m_1^2
   \]
   Rearrange to form quadratic equation:
   \[
   2m_1^2 + 3m_1 + 1 = 0
   \]
   
9. **Solve the Negative Quadratic Equation:**
   Using quadratic formula,  
   \( m_1 = \frac{-3 \pm \sqrt{9 - 8}}{4} = \frac{-3 \pm 1}{4} \)  
   So,
   \( m_1 = -0.5 \) or \( m_1 = -1 \).

10. **Find Corresponding \( m_2 \) Values:**
    If \( m_1 = -0.5 \), then \( m_2 = 2(-0.5) = -1 \).  
    If \( m_1 = -1 \), then \( m_2 = 2(-1) = -2 \).

**Final Answer:**

The possible slopes of the two lines are:
- (m₁, m₂) = (1, 2)
- (m₁, m₂) = (0.5, 1)
- (m₁, m₂) = (-0.5, -1)
- (m₁, m₂) = (-1, -2)

To determine the equation of a line with a slope (�लान) of \(-2\) that passes through the point \((0,\ 5)\), we can use the **slope-intercept form** of a linear equation:

\[
y = mx + b
\]

where:
- \(m\) is the slope (\(-2\) in this case),
- \(b\) is the y-intercept.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Identify the given values:**
   - Slope (\(m\)) = \(-2\)
   - Point through which the line passes = \((0,\ 5)\)

2. **Plug the slope into the equation:**

   \[
   y = (-2)x + b
   \]

3. **Use the given point to find \(b\) (the y-intercept):**
   
   Since the point \((0,\ 5)\) lies on the line, substitute \(x = 0\) and \(y = 5\) into the equation:

   \[
   5 = (-2)(0) + b
   \]
   
   Simplify:
   
   \[
   5 = 0 + b \\
   b = 5
   \]

4. **Write the final equation of the line:**

   Substitute \(b = 5\) back into the slope-intercept form:

   \[
   y = -2x + 5
   \]

### Final Answer:
\[
\boxed{y=-2x+5}
\]

To find the equation of the line passing through the points (3, 1) and (5, 7), we can follow these steps:

**Step 1: Calculate the Slope**

The slope \( m \) between two points \( (x_1, y_1) \) and \( (x_2, y_2) \) is given by:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
\]
Substituting the given points:
\[
m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
\]

**Step 2: Use Point-Slope Form**

The point-slope form of a line is:
\[
y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
\]
Using the point (3, 1):
\[
y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
\]

**Step 3: Simplify to Slope-Intercept Form**

Expanding and simplifying:
\[
y - 1 = 3x - 9 \\
y = 3x - 8
\]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{y = 3x - 8}
\]

To determine the equation of a line with \(\tan(\theta) = \frac{1}{6}\), we proceed as follows:

1. **Understanding the Slope:**
   - The slope \(m\) of a line is given by \(m = \tan(\theta)\).
   - Therefore, if \(\tan(\theta) = \frac{1}{6}\), then the slope \(m = \frac{1}{6}\).

2. **General Equation of the Line:**
   - The equation of a line in slope-intercept form is:
     \[
     y = mx + c
     \]
     where \(c\) is the y-intercept.

3. **Incorporating the Slope:**
   - Substituting \(m = \frac{1}{6}\) into the equation, we get:
     \[
     y = \frac{1}{6}x + c
     \]
   
4. **Finding the X-Intercept:**
   - The x-intercept occurs where \(y = 0\):
     \[
     0 = \frac{1}{6}x + c \implies x = -6c
     \]
   - Thus, the x-intercept is at the point \((-6c, 0)\).

5. **Conclusion:**
   - The general equation of any line with \(\tan(\theta) = \frac{1}{6}\) (slope \(m = \frac{1}{6}\)) is:
     \[
     y = \frac{1}{6}x + c
     \]
     where \(c\) can be any real number, determining the specific line within this family.

**Final Answer:**
All such lines have equations of the form 
\[
y = \frac{1}{6}x + c
\]
where \(c\) is a constant representing the y-intercept.

The section formula for internal division is:
R(x) = (m*Bx + n*Ax)/(m + n)
R(y) = (m*By + n*Ay)/(m + n)

Plugging in the values:
m = 3, n = 4
Ax = 1, Ay = 2
Bx = 4, By = 6

Rx = (3*4 + 4*1)/(3+4) = (12 + 4)/7 = 16/7 ≈ 2.2857
Ry = (3*6 + 4*2)/(3+4) = (18 + 8)/7 = 26/7 ≈ 3.7143

Multiply both sides by 3 to eliminate the fraction:

= (-14/7) + 2
= (-2) + 2
= 0

To determine the linear demand function based on the given "at least" conditions, we proceed by using the two data points (114, 1080) and (116, 1320). 

1. **Calculate the slope (m):**
   \[
   m = \frac{Q_2 - Q_1}{P_2 - P_1} = \frac{1320 - 1080}{116 - 114} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
   \]

2. **Determine the intercept (b) using one of the points:**
   Using point (114, 1080):
   \[
   1080 = 120(114) + b \\
   1080 = 13680 + b \\
   b = 1080 - 13680 = -12600
   \]

Thus, the linear demand function is:
\[
Q = 120P - 12600
\]

However, it's important to note that in a typical demand scenario, higher prices should correspond to lower quantities demanded. The derived function suggests otherwise, which might imply an error in interpreting "at least" as indicating the minimum quantity sold at specific price points. For practical purposes and following the given instructions, we'll accept this linear relationship.

**Final Answer:**
The linear function is \boxed{Q = 120P - 12600}.

To find the equation of the line passing through the point \((0, 2)\) and making an angle of \(2\frac{2}{3}\) radians with the positive x-axis, follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**
   
   The slope of a line is given by the tangent of the angle it makes with the positive x-axis.
   
   \[
   m = \tan(2\frac{2}{3}) 
   \]
   
   First, convert \(2\frac{2}{3}\) to an improper fraction:
   
   \[
   2\frac{2}{3} = \frac{8}{3} \text{ radians}
   \]
   
   Now, compute the tangent:
   
   \[
   m = \tan\left(\frac{8}{3}\right)
   \]
   
   Using a calculator (assuming standard trigonometric values):
   
   \[
   m ≈ -0.6947
   \]

2. **Determine the Equation of the Line:**
   
   The line passes through the point \((0, 2)\), which is the y-intercept (\(b = 2\)).
   
   Using the slope-intercept form \(y = mx + b\):
   
   \[
   y = -0.6947x + 2
   \]
   
3. **Find a Line Parallel to It:**
   
   A line parallel to the given line will have the same slope (\(m ≈ -0.6947\)).
   
   For example, any line of the form:
   
   \[
   y = -0.6947x + c
   \]
   
   where \(c\) is a constant different from 2.
   
4. **Determine Where It Crosses the Y-Axis:**
   
   The y-intercept occurs when \(x = 0\). Substituting into the equation:
   
   \[
   y = -0.6947(0) + c = c
   \]
   
   So, it crosses the y-axis at \((0, c)\).
   
**Final Answer:**

The equation of the line is:

\[
y = -0.695x + 2
\]

A parallel line would have the same slope and intersect the y-axis at a different point, such as \(y = -0.695x + c\) where \(c \neq 2\).

To find the equation of the line that intersects both axes at the same point and passes through (4, 5), follow these steps:

1. **Understand the Problem:**
   - The line intersects the x-axis at \((a, 0)\) and the y-axis at \((0, a)\).

2. **Use the Intercept Form of the Equation:**
   
   The general equation for intercepts on both axes is:
   \[
   \frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
   \]
   
   Since \(a = b\), this simplifies to:
   \[
   x + y = a
   \]

3. **Substitute the Given Point (4, 5) into the Equation:**
   
   Plugging in \(x = 4\) and \(y = 5\):
   \[
   4 + 5 = a \\
   a = 9
   \]

4. **Write the Final Equation of the Line:**

   Substituting \(a = 9\) into \(x + y = a\):
   \[
   x + y = 9
   \]
   
   Therefore, the equation of the line is:
   \[
   \boxed{x + y = 9}
   \]

The equation of the line passing through the points (4,7) and (-5,8) is found by first calculating the slope:

Slope \( m = \frac{8 - 7}{-5 - 4} = -\frac{1}{9} \)

Using point-slope form with one point (e.g., (4,7)):

\( y - 7 = -\frac{1}{9}(x - 4) \)

Simplifying to slope-intercept form:

\( y = -\frac{1}{9}x + \frac{67}{9} \)

For the perpendicular line, its slope will be the negative reciprocal of \( -\frac{1}{9} \), which is 9. Using point (4,7):

Perpendicular Line: \( y - 7 = 9(x - 4) \)
Simplifying:
\( y = 9x - 29 \)

So the JSON containing both lines' equations and slopes:

The formula to find the distance between two parallel lines given by \( Ax + By + C_1 = 0 \) and \( Ax + By + C_2 = 0 \) is:

\[
d = \frac{|C_1 - C_2|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Applying this formula to the given lines:
- For the first line, \( 3x - 4y + 12 = 0 \), we have \( A = 3 \), \( B = -4 \), and \( C_1 = 12 \).
- For the second line, \( 3x - 4y + 7 = 0 \), we have \( C_2 = 7 \).

Substituting these values into the formula:

\[
d = \frac{|12 - 7|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}} = \frac{5}{5} = 1
\]

The distance between the two lines is 1.

{Distance Between Two Lines: 1}

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the Point Form:** Any point on the x-axis has coordinates (a, 0), where 'a' is unknown.

2. **Rewrite Line Equation:** Convert \( \frac{x}{3} + \frac{y}{4} = 1 \) to standard form by multiplying both sides by 12:
   - Resulting equation: 4x + 3y = 12
   - Standard form: 4x + 3y - 12 = 0

3. **Apply Distance Formula:** Use the distance formula from a point (a, 0) to the line:
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \frac{|4a + 3(0) - 12|}{\sqrt{4^2 + 3^2}} = \frac{|4a - 12|}{5}
   \]

4. **Set Distance Equal to 4:**
   \[
   \frac{|4a - 12|}{5} = 4
   \]
   
5. **Solve Absolute Value Equation:**
   - Case 1: \(4a - 12 = 20\) → \(a = 8\)
   - Case 2: \(4a - 12 = -20\) → \(a = -2\)

6. **Conclusion:** The points are (8, 0) and (-2, 0).

To find the distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line given by \(x + 12 = 10(y - 4)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Rewrite the Line Equation in Standard Form
The standard form of a line is \(Ax + By + C = 0\).

Starting with:
\[
x + 12 = 10(y - 4)
\]
Expand and rearrange terms to get it into standard form:
\[
x + 12 = 10y - 40 \\
x - 10y + 52 = 0
\]
So, \(A = 1\), \(B = -10\), and \(C = 52\).

### Step 2: Use the Distance Formula
The distance \(d\) from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is given by:
\[
d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]
Substitute the known values into the formula:
\[
d = \frac{|1(-2) + (-10)(2) + 52|}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-10)^2}} \\
= \frac{|-2 - 20 + 52|}{\sqrt{1 + 100}} \\
= \frac{|30|}{\sqrt{101}} \\
= \frac{30}{\sqrt{101}}
\]

The distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line is:

\boxed{\dfrac{30}{\sqrt{101}}}

**Solution:**

To determine the slope (m) of the line passing through the points \((6, 4)\) and \((-2, 8)\), we can use the slope formula:

\[

\]

- Let \((x_1, y_1) = (6, 4)\)
- Let \((x_2, y_2) = (-2, 8)\)

**Step 2:** Plug in the values into the slope formula.

\[
m = \frac{8 - 4}{-2 - 6}
\]

**Step 3:** Perform the subtraction.

\[
m = \frac{4}{-8} = -0.5
\]

First, I need to determine the slope of a line that makes an angle of 120 degrees with the positive direction of the x-axis. The slope of a line is related to the tangent of the angle it makes with the x-axis.

Since 120 degrees lies in the second quadrant where angles are measured from 180 degrees, I can find its reference angle by subtracting it from 180 degrees:
Reference angle = 180° - 120° = 60°

Now, calculate the tangent of this reference angle to find the slope:

tan(60°) is equal to √3.

Therefore, the slope of the line is √3.

**Solution:**

To determine the slope (\( m \)) of a line that makes an angle of \( 120^\circ \) with the positive direction of the x-axis:

1. **Understand the Relationship Between Slope and Angle:**
   
   The slope of a line is given by:
   \[
   m = \tan(\theta)
   \]
   where \( \theta \) is the angle made by the line with the positive direction of the x-axis.

2. **Calculate the Tangent of 120 Degrees:**

   Since \( 120^\circ \) lies in the second quadrant, we can find its reference angle:
   \[
   \text{Reference angle} = 180^\circ - 120^\circ = 60^\circ
   \]
   
   Now, calculate the tangent of this reference angle:
   \[
   m = \tan(120^\circ) = \tan(60^\circ)
   \]

3. **Find \( \tan(60^\circ) \):**
   
   We know that:
   \[
   \tan(60^\circ) = \sqrt{3}
   \]
   
4. **Final Answer:**

   Therefore, the slope of the line is:
   \[
   m = \sqrt{3}
   \]

**Answer:** {Final Answer: "√3"}

Okay, so I have this problem where there's an angle between two lines, and one of them has a slope of 1/4. The angle is π/4 radians, which is 45 degrees. I need to find the slope of the second line.

Hmm, I remember that when you have two lines with slopes m1 and m2, the angle θ between them can be found using the formula:

tanθ = |(m2 - m1)/(1 + m1*m2)|

In this case, θ is π/4, so tanθ would be 1. The given slope m1 is 1/4. So I can plug these into the formula.

Let me write that down: tan(π/4) = |(m2 - 1/4)/(1 + (1/4)*m2)|

Since tan(π/4) is 1, it simplifies to:

1 = |(m2 - 1/4)/(1 + m2/4)|

Now I need to solve for m2. Because of the absolute value, this equation will have two cases: one where the expression inside is positive and another where it's negative.

Case 1:
(m2 - 1/4) / (1 + m2/4) = 1

Multiply both sides by denominator:

m2 - 1/4 = 1*(1 + m2/4)

Expand the right side:

m2 - 1/4 = 1 + m2/4

Now, let's get all terms involving m2 on one side and constants on the other.

Subtract m2/4 from both sides:

(3/4)m2 - 1/4 = 1

Add 1/4 to both sides:

(3/4)m2 = 5/4

Multiply both sides by 4/3:

m2 = (5/4)*(4/3) = 5/3

Case 2:
(m2 - 1/4) / (1 + m2/4) = -1

Again, multiply both sides by denominator:

m2 - 1/4 = -1*(1 + m2/4)

Expand right side:

m2 - 1/4 = -1 - m2/4

Bring all terms with m2 to one side and constants to the other.

Add m2/4 to both sides:

(5/4)m2 - 1/4 = -1

Add 1/4 to both sides:

To find the equation of the line passing through the points \((3, 1)\) and \((5, 7)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Calculate the Slope (m)
The slope \( m \) is given by:
\[
m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3

Using one of the points, say \((3, 1)\), and the slope \( m = 3 \) in the point-slope formula:
\[

\]
\[
y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
\]

\[

\[
y = 3x - 8
\]

   - The general equation is \( y = mx + b \),
     where:
     - \( m \) is the slope,
     - \( b \) is the y-intercept.

3. **Substitute the known values:**
   - Slope (\( m \)) = \(\frac{1}{4}\)

   \[
   y = \left(\frac{1}{4}\right)x - \frac{5}{4}
   \]

\[ y = \boxed{\dfrac{x}{4} - \dfrac{5}{4}} \]

To determine whether the three points \((3, 0)\), \((-2, -2)\), and \((8, 2)\) are **collinear** (lying on the same straight line), we can use the concept of **slope** or the **area of a triangle** formed by these points. Here, I'll use the slope method.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Calculate the Slope between Points A and B:**
   
   \[
   m_{AB} = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{-2 - 0}{-2 - 3} = \frac{-2}{-5} = \frac{2}{5}
   \]

2. **Calculate the Slope between Points B and C:**
   
   \[
   m_{BC} = \frac{y_3 - y_2}{x_3 - x_2} = \frac{2 - (-2)}{8 - (-2)} = \frac{4}{10} = \frac{2}{5}
   \]

3. **Compare the Slopes:**
   
   Since both slopes \( m_{AB} \) and \( m_{BC} \) are equal (\(\frac{2}{5}\)), this indicates that all three points lie on the same straight line.

### Conclusion:

The three points \((3, 0)\), \((-2, -2)\), and \((8, 2)\) are **collinear** because the slopes between each pair of points are equal.

- At P = ₹114, Q = 1080 liters
- At P = ₹116, Q = 1320 liters

\[ m = \frac{Q_2 - Q_1}{P_2 - P_1} = \frac{1320 - 1080}{116 - 114} = \frac{240}{2} = 120 \]

\[ Q = mP + b \]

\[ 1080 = 120(114) + b \]
\[ 1080 = 13680 + b \]
\[ b = 1080 - 13680 = -12600 \]

Thus, the demand equation is:
\[ Q = 120P - 12600 \]

At P = ₹117:
\[ Q = 120(117) - 12600 = 14040 - 12600 = 1440 \]

**Quantity Demanded at ₹117 per liter is 1,440 liters.**

1. Calculate the slope (m) using two given points.
2. Use point-slope form to find the intercept (b).
3. Formulate the demand equation Q = mP + b.
4. Substitute P = 117 into the equation to find Q.

y = -1.4966x + 2
\]

Now, to find a line parallel to this one that intersects the y-axis at \( (0, -2) \), I use the same slope since parallel lines have identical slopes. Thus, plugging into the slope-intercept form:
\[
y = -1.4966x - 2
\]

In summary, the equation of the original line is \( y = -1.4966x + 2 \), and its parallel counterpart that intersects the y-axis at (0, -2) has the equation \( y = -1.4966x - 2 \).

To find the equations of the lines as described:

### Given:
- Point through which both lines pass: **(0, 2)**.
- The line forms an angle of \(2 \frac{2}{3}\) radians with the positive x-axis.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**
   - The slope \( m \) is equal to the tangent of the given angle.
   \[
   m = \tan\left(2 \frac{2}{3}\right) = \tan\left(\frac{8}{3}\right)
   \]
   - Using a calculator, we find:
     \[
     \tan\left(\frac{8}{3}\right) \approx -1.4966
     \]

2. **Determine the Equation of the First Line:**
   - The general form of the equation is \( y = mx + b \).
   - Since the line passes through (0, 2), this point gives the y-intercept \( b = 2 \).
   - Substituting the values:
     \[
     y = -1.4966x + 2
     \]

3. **Find the Equation of the Parallel Line:**
   - Parallel lines have the same slope.
   - The new line passes through (0, -2), so this point gives a new y-intercept \( b' = -2 \).
   - Substituting into the equation:
     \[
     y = -1.4966x - 2
     \]

Given the problem of finding the equation of a line passing through the point (4, 4) with intercepts whose sum is 13, we start by considering the general form of a line in intercept form:

\[
\frac{x}{a} + \frac{y}{b} = 1
\]

Since the line passes through (4, 4), substituting these values gives:

\[
\frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{b} = 1
\]

We are also given that the sum of the intercepts is 13, so:

\[
a + b = 13
\]

Expressing \(b\) in terms of \(a\), we get \(b = 13 - a\). Substituting this into the equation from the point (4, 4):

\[
\frac{4}{a} + \frac{4}{13 - a} = 1
\]

Combining these fractions:

\[
\frac{4(13 - a) + 4a}{a(13 - a)} = 1
\]

Simplifying the numerator:

\[
\frac{52 - 4a + 4a}{a(13 - a)} = 1 \implies \frac{52}{a(13 - a)} = 1
\]

This leads to:

\[
a(13 - a) = 52
\]

Expanding and rearranging terms:

\[
13a - a^2 = 52 \implies a^2 - 13a + 52 = 0
\]

Solving this quadratic equation using the discriminant:

\[
D = (-13)^2 - 4 \cdot 1 \cdot 52 = 169 - 208 = -39
\]

Since the discriminant is negative, there are no real solutions for \(a\) and \(b\). This implies that there is no line passing through (4, 4) with intercepts whose sum is 13.

Given this information, it is clear that more details or constraints are needed to determine a unique solution. Therefore, the equation of the line cannot be determined as it stands.

### Final Answer
The equation of the line cannot be determined with the given information because only one point is provided. Additional information such as another point or the slope is needed to find a unique solution.

\boxed{y = mx + b}

To find the equation of a line perpendicular to the segment connecting (1,0) and (2,3), with slope -1/3. There are two such lines passing through each endpoint:

- For point (1,0): y = (-1/3)x + 1/3
- For point (2,3): y = (-1/3)x + 11/3

These are the two possible equations of the perpendicular lines.

Step-by-step explanation:
1. Calculate the slope of the original segment: m = (3 - 0)/(2 - 1) = 3.
2. The slope of the perpendicular line is the negative reciprocal: m_perp = -1/3.
3. Using point-slope form for each endpoint to find their respective equations:
   - For (1,0): y - 0 = (-1/3)(x - 1) → y = (-1/3)x + 1/3.
   - For (2,3): y - 3 = (-1/3)(x - 2) → y = (-1/3)x + 11/3.

Answer: The equations of the perpendicular lines are \boxed{y = -\frac{1}{3}x + \frac{1}{3}} and \boxed{y = -\frac{1}{3}x + \frac{11}{3}}.

**Solution:**

We are given:
- The slope (\(m\)) of the line is \(-4\).
- The line intersects the x-axis at \(5\) units to the left of the origin, which means it passes through the point \((-5, 0)\).

Using the **point-slope form** of a linear equation:
\[ y - k = m(x - h) \]
where \((h, k)\) is a point on the line.

Substituting \(m = -4\), and the point \((-5, 0)\):
\[
y - 0 = -4(x - (-5)) \\
y = -4(x + 5)
\]

Simplifying:
\[
y = -4x - 20
\]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{y=-4x-20}
\]

First, I recognize that the problem requires finding the distance from a specific point to a given line.

The general formula for calculating the distance (d) from a point (x₀, y₀) to the line Ax + By + C = 0 is:

\[ d = \frac{|A x₀ + B y₀ + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \]

Given the line equation \(5x - 6y - 28 = 0\) and the point (5, -7), I will substitute these values into the formula.

Next, I'll compute the numerator by plugging in x₀ = 5 and y₀ = -7:

\[ |5(5) + (-6)(-7) - 28| \]

Calculating each term:
- \(5 * 5 = 25\)
- \(-6 * -7 = 42\)
Adding these together with the constant term: 
\[ 25 + 42 - 28 = 39 \]
Taking the absolute value gives:
\[ |39| = 39 \]

Now, I'll compute the denominator by finding the square root of the sum of squares of A and B:

\[\sqrt{5^2 + (-6)^2}\]

Calculating each term:
- \(5^2 = 25\)
- \((-6)^2 = 36\)

Adding these together and taking the square root:
\[ \sqrt{25 + 36} = \sqrt{61} \]

Finally, I'll divide the numerator by the denominator to find the distance:

\[ d = \frac{39}{\sqrt{61}} \]

This simplifies to approximately 4.97 when calculated numerically.

The formula used to calculate the distance between two parallel lines \( ax + by + c_1 = 0 \) and \( ax + by + c_2 = 0 \) is:

\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|c_1 - c_2|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}}
\]

Applying this to the given lines:
- For the first line, \( 3x - 4y + 12 = 0 \), we have \( a = 3 \), \( b = -4 \), and \( c_1 = 12 \).
- For the second line, \( 3x - 4y + 7 = 0 \), we have \( a = 3 \), \( b = -4 \), and \( c_2 = 7 \).

Calculating the numerator:
\[
|c_1 - c_2| = |12 - 7| = 5
\]

Calculating the denominator:
\[
\sqrt{a^2 + b^2} = \sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2} = \sqrt{9 + 16} = \sqrt{25} = 5
\]

Thus, the distance between the two lines is:
\[
\frac{5}{5} = 1
\]

To find the equation of the line passing through points **B (6, 1)** and **C (3, 4)**:

1. **Calculate the slope (m):**
   
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} = \frac{4 - 1}{3 - 6} = \frac{3}{-3} = -1
   \]

   Using point B (6, 1):
   
   \[
   y - 1 = -1(x - 6) \\
   y - 1 = -x + 6 \\
   y = -x + 7
   \]

   \[
   x + y - 7 = 0
   \]

   \[

   d = \frac{|1 \cdot 4 + 1 \cdot 5 - 7|}{\sqrt{1^2 + 1^2}} \\

The values of \( m \) and \( c \) for the line \( y = mx + c \), given that it is perpendicular to a line from the origin passing through (2,4), are found as follows:

1. The slope between the points (0,0) and (2,4) is 2.
2. Since this is the negative reciprocal of the original line's slope \( m \), we have:
   \[
   -\frac{1}{m} = 2 \Rightarrow m = -\frac{1}{2}
   \]
3. Substituting \( m = -\frac{1}{2} \) and the point (2,4) into the line equation:
   \[
   4 = -\frac{1}{2}(2) + c \Rightarrow 4 = -1 + c \Rightarrow c = 5
   \]

The slope \( m \) is \(-\frac{1}{2}\) and the intercept \( c \) is \(5\).

To determine the value of \( H \), we use the condition for two lines to be perpendicular: their slopes multiply to -1.

1. **Find the slope of the given line**:  
   The equation is \( 7x - 9y - 19 = 0 \). Rearranging to slope-intercept form (\( y = mx + b \)):  
   \[
   -9y = -7x + 19 \implies y = \frac{7}{9}x - \frac{19}{9}
   \]  
   So, the slope \( m_1 = \frac{7}{9} \).

2. **Determine the perpendicular slope**:  
   The slope of a line perpendicular to this is the negative reciprocal:  
   \[
   m_2 = -\frac{9}{7}
   \]

3. **Calculate the slope between points \( (H, 5) \) and \( (6, 3) \)**:  
   Using the formula for slope (\( \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} \)):  
   \[
   m = \frac{3 - 5}{6 - H} = \frac{-2}{6 - H}
   \]  

4. **Set this equal to the perpendicular slope and solve for \( H \)**:  
   \[
   \frac{-2}{6 - H} = -\frac{9}{7}
   \]

   \[
   (-2) \cdot 7 = (-9)(6 - H) \\
   -14 = -54 + 9H \\
   40 = 9H \\
   H = \frac{40}{9}
   \]

Since both lines have the same slope, they are parallel.

Next, I use the formula for the distance between two parallel lines:
\[ d = \frac{|C_2 - C_1|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}} \]

First line: \(19x + 12y - 38 = 0\) can be rewritten as \(19x + 12y + (-38) = 0\).
Second line: \(38x + 24y + 70 = 0\) can be simplified by dividing all terms by 2, resulting in \(19x + 12y + 35 = 0\).

Now, applying the values to the formula:
\[ d = \frac{|35 - (-38)|}{\sqrt{19^2 + 12^2}} = \frac{|73|}{\sqrt{361 + 144}} = \frac{73}{\sqrt{505}} \]

Calculating the square root of 505:
\[ \sqrt{505} ≈ 22.47 \]

Finally, dividing 73 by approximately 22.47 gives:
\[ d ≈ 3.25 \]

To find the distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line defined by \(x + 12 = 10(y - 4)\), follow these steps:

**Step 1: Rewrite the Line Equation in Standard Form**

Starting with:
\[
x + 12 = 10(y - 4)
\]

Expand and rearrange terms:
\[
x + 12 = 10y - 40 \\
x - 10y + 52 = 0
\]
So, the standard form is \(x - 10y + 52 = 0\), where:
- \(A = 1\)
- \(B = -10\)
- \(C = 52\)

**Step 2: Apply the Distance Formula**

The distance from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is:
\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]

Substitute \(A = 1\), \(B = -10\), \(C = 52\), and the point \((-2, 2)\):
\[
\text{Distance} = \frac{|1 \cdot (-2) + (-10) \cdot 2 + 52|}{\sqrt{1^2 + (-10)^2}} \\
= \frac{|-2 - 20 + 52|}{\sqrt{1 + 100}} \\
= \frac{|30|}{\sqrt{101}} \\
= \frac{30}{\sqrt{101}}
\]

**Step 3: Simplify the Expression**

To rationalize the denominator:
\[
\frac{30}{\sqrt{101}} = \frac{30 \cdot \sqrt{101}}{101} \\
= \frac{30\sqrt{101}}{101}
\]

**Final Answer:**

The distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line is:
\[
\boxed{\frac{30\sqrt{101}}{101}}
\]

The distance from point P(4, 1) to the line 4x - y = 0 is calculated using the formula for the distance from a point to a line. Here's how:

1. **Formula**: The distance \( d \) from a point \( (x_0, y_0) \) to the line \( Ax + By + C = 0 \) is given by:
   \[
   d = \frac{|Ax_0 + By_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
   \]
   
2. **Identify A, B, C**: From the equation \( 4x - y = 0 \), we have \( A = 4 \), \( B = -1 \), and \( C = 0 \).

3. **Plug in the values**:
   \[
   d = \frac{|4(4) + (-1)(1) + 0|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2}} = \frac{|16 - 1|}{\sqrt{16 + 1}} = \frac{15}{\sqrt{17}}
   \]

4. **Rationalize if needed**: 
   \[
   d = \frac{15\sqrt{17}}{17}
   \]

**Solution:**

To determine the slope of a line that forms an angle of \(60^\circ\) with the positive y-axis (measured anticlockwise), follow these steps:

1. **Understand the Angle Orientation:**
   - Normally, angles are measured from the positive x-axis in the anticlockwise direction.
   - Here, the angle is given with respect to the positive y-axis.

2. **Convert the Given Angle to Standard Position:**
   - The line makes an angle of \(60^\circ\) above the negative x-axis (since it's moving anticlockwise from the y-axis).
   - Therefore, its position relative to the standard x-axis is \(180^\circ - 60^\circ = 120^\circ\).

3. **Calculate the Slope:**
   - The slope (\(m\)) of a line is equal to the tangent of the angle it makes with the positive x-axis.
   - So, \( m = \tan(120^\circ) \).
   - Using trigonometric values:
     \[
     \tan(120^\circ) = \tan(180^\circ - 60^\circ) = -\tan(60^\circ) = -\sqrt{3}
     \]

The problem involves finding all possible real numbers \( m \) such that there exist two lines with slopes \( m \) and another slope (which we can denote as \( k = 2m \)) have an angle between them whose tangent is \( \frac{1}{3} \).

We use the formula for the tangent of the angle \( \theta \) between two lines with slopes \( m_1 \) and \( m_2 \):
\[
\tan(\theta) = \left| \frac{m_2 - m_1}{1 + m_1 m_2} \right|
\]
Here, since we're considering the case where one slope is \( m \) and the other is \( 2m \), we substitute these into the formula:
\[
\tan(\theta) = \left| \frac{2m - m}{1 + m(2m)} \right| = \left| \frac{m}{1 + 2m^2} \right|
\]
Given that \( \tan(\theta) = \frac{1}{3} \), we set up the equation:
\[
\left| \frac{m}{1 + 2m^2} \right| = \frac{1}{3}
\]
Solving this absolute value equation involves squaring both sides to eliminate the absolute value and then solving for \( m \):
\[
\left( \frac{m}{1 + 2m^2} \right)^2 = \left( \frac{1}{3} \right)^2 \\
\Rightarrow \frac{m^2}{(1 + 2m^2)^2} = \frac{1}{9}
\]
Multiplying both sides by \( (1 + 2m^2)^2 \) and simplifying leads to a quartic equation in terms of \( m^2 \), which can be solved using substitution, resulting in the possible real solutions for \( m \).

The final values are:
\[
m = -1,\,-0.5,\,0.5,\,1
\]

**Answer:**

The possible values of \( m \) are \( -1, -\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2}, 1 \).

- **Slope (m)** = -2
- **Point** = (0, 5)

Since the point is on the y-axis (x = 0), it directly gives us the **y-intercept (b)** as 5.

***********************<think>
To find the equation of a line passing through two points, first calculate the slope using the formula (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1). With the given points (3, 1) and (5, 7), the slope is (7 - 1)/(5 - 3) which equals 6/2 or 3.

Next, use the point-slope form of a linear equation: y - y1 = m(x - x1). Substituting one of the points into this formula gives y - 1 = 3(x - 3).

Finally, simplify the equation to get it in standard form. Distribute the slope and then rearrange terms to obtain 3x - y = 8.
</think>

**Solution:**

To find the equation of the line passing through the points (3, 1) and (5, 7), follow these steps:

1. **Calculate the Slope (m):**
   
   The slope \( m \) is given by:
   \[
   m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1}
   \]
   
   Substituting the points:
   \[
   m = \frac{7 - 1}{5 - 3} = \frac{6}{2} = 3
   \]

2. **Use the Point-Slope Form:**
   
   The point-slope form of a line is:
   \[
   y - y_1 = m(x - x_1)
   \]
   
   Using the point (3, 1):
   \[
   y - 1 = 3(x - 3)
   \]

3. **Simplify to Standard Form:**
   
   Expand and rearrange the equation:
   \[
   y - 1 = 3x - 9
   \]
   \[
   y = 3x - 8
   \]
   
   Bringing all terms to one side:
   \[
   3x - y = 8
   \]

**Final Answer:**
\[
\boxed{3x - y = 8}
\]

To solve the problem of finding the equation of a line that divides a given segment in a 1:4 ratio, follow these steps:

1. **Identify Coordinates**: Determine the coordinates of the endpoints A and B of the segment.

2. **Apply Section Formula**:
   - If R divides AB internally in the ratio m:n (here, m = 1 and n = 4), use the section formula to find the coordinates of R.
   - The formula is: 
     \[
     R\left(\frac{m x_2 + n x_1}{m + n}, \frac{m y_2 + n y_1}{m + n}\right)
     \]
   
3. **Determine Equation**: With the coordinates of R, use two-point form or another method to find the equation of the line.

**Example Calculation:**

Suppose endpoint A is (x₁, y₁) and endpoint B is (x₂, y₂).

Using m = 1 and n = 4:

- x-coordinate of R:
  \[
  \frac{1 \cdot x_2 + 4 \cdot x_1}{1 + 4} = \frac{x_2 + 4x_1}{5}
  \]
  
- y-coordinate of R:
  \[
  \frac{1 \cdot y_2 + 4 \cdot y_1}{5}
  \]

Now, with points A and R (or another suitable pair), compute the slope and use point-slope form to write the equation.

The problem involves determining how many liters of a product can be sold at a certain price based on two given points. We model this relationship linearly.

**Step-by-Step Explanation:**

1. **Identify the Points:**
   - When the price (p) is 114, the quantity sold (q) is 1080 liters.
   - When the price increases to 116, the quantity sold increases to 1320 liters.

2. **Calculate the Slope of the Line:**
   - The slope (m) between two points (p₁, q₁) and (p₂, q₂) is given by:
     \[
     m = \frac{q₂ - q₁}{p₂ - p₁} = \frac{1320 - 1080}{116 - 114} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
     \]

3. **Formulate the Linear Equation:**
   Using one of the points, say (114, 1080), substitute into the equation \( q = mp + b \):
   \[
   1080 = 120(114) + b
   \]
   Calculate:
   \[
   1080 = 13680 + b \\
   b = 1080 - 13680 \\
   b = -12600
   \]
   
   Thus, the equation is:
   \[
   q = 120p - 12600
   \]

4. **Find Quantity at p=117:**
   Substitute \( p = 117 \) into the equation:
   \[
   q = 120(117) - 12600 \\
   q = 14040 - 12600 \\
   q = 1440
   \]

**Answer:**
The number of liters sold at a price of ₹117 per liter is **1,440 liters**.

I need to determine the equation of a line that passes through the point (4, 4) and has an intercept sum of 13. Let's break this down step by step.

First, recall the standard form of a linear equation in slope-intercept form:
y = mx + b

Here, m represents the slope of the line, and b is the y-intercept where the line crosses the y-axis at (0, b).

The problem states that the sum of the intercepts is 13. Since this is a linear equation with only two terms (mx and b), there's no x-intercept unless m is zero, which would make it a horizontal line.

Assuming the line has both x and y intercepts, let's denote the x-intercept as 'a'. This means the line crosses the x-axis at (a, 0). Similarly, we already have the y-intercept at (0, b), so the sum of these intercepts is a + b = 13.

Now, substituting the point (4, 4) into the equation:
4 = m*4 + b

But since I also know that a + b = 13 and the x-intercept occurs where y=0, plugging this in gives:
0 = m*a + b
=> b = -m*a

Substituting back into the sum of intercepts:
a + (-m*a) = 13
a(1 - m) = 13

Now I have two equations:
1. 4 = 4m + b
2. a(1 - m) = 13

From equation 1, solve for b:
b = 4 - 4m

Substitute this into the sum of intercepts equation (a + b = 13):
a + (4 - 4m) = 13
a = 13 - 4 + 4m
a = 9 + 4m

Now substitute a back into equation from the x-intercept:
(9 + 4m)(1 - m) = 13

Expanding this:
9*(1 - m) + 4m*(1 - m) = 13
9 - 9m + 4m - 4m² = 13
Combine like terms:
-5m -4m² + 9 = 13
Rearrange to form a quadratic equation:
-4m² -5m -4 = 0

Multiply through by -1 to simplify:
4m² +5m +4 = 0

Now, solve for m using the quadratic formula:
m = [-b ± sqrt(b² -4ac)]/(2a)
Here, a=4, b=5, c=4
Discriminant D = 5² - 4*4*4 = 25 -64 = -39

Since the discriminant is negative, there are no real solutions for m. This implies that it's impossible to have such a line with both x and y intercepts adding up to 13 while passing through (4, 4).

Therefore, under the given conditions, the problem does not have a solution.

The equation of the line perpendicular to the segment connecting (1,0) and (2,3), passing through its midpoint is y = (-1/3)x + 2.

Step-by-step explanation:

1. **Find the slope of the original segment:**
   - Two points are given: A(1,0) and B(2,3).
   - Slope (m₁) = (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁) = (3 - 0)/(2 - 1) = 3/1 = 3.

2. **Determine the slope of the perpendicular line:**
   - The negative reciprocal of m₁ is m₂.
   - So, m₂ = -1/m₁ = -1/3.

3. **Find the midpoint of AB for the perpendicular bisector:**
   - Midpoint M has coordinates:
     - x = (x₁ + x₂)/2 = (1 + 2)/2 = 1.5
     - y = (y₁ + y₂)/2 = (0 + 3)/2 = 1.5
   - So, midpoint M is (1.5, 1.5).

4. **Use point-slope form to find the equation of the perpendicular bisector:**
   - Using slope m₂ = -1/3 and point M(1.5, 1.5).
     y - 1.5 = (-1/3)(x - 1.5)

5. **Simplify to slope-intercept form (y = mx + b):**
   - Distribute the slope: y - 1.5 = (-1/3)x + (1.5)*(1/3) => y - 1.5 = (-1/3)x + 0.5
   - Add 1.5 to both sides: y = (-1/3)x + 2

**Final Answer:**  
The equation of the perpendicular line is **y = (-1/3)x + 2**.

So plugging in the points (4,7) as (x1,y1) and (-5,8) as (x2,y2), we get:

m = (8 - 7)/(-5 - 4) = (1)/(-9) = -1/9.

y - 7 = (-1/9)(x - 4).

Now, for the perpendicular bisector. A perpendicular bisector is a line that intersects the original line at its midpoint and has a slope that is the negative reciprocal of the original slope.

To determine the distance between two parallel lines, I'll use the formula for the distance from a point to a line. Since both lines are identical except for their constants, I can choose any point on one line and calculate its distance to the other line.

To find the **distance between two parallel lines** given by the equations:

\[
3x - 4y + 12 = 0
\]
\[
3x - 4y + 7 = 0
\]

we can use the formula for the distance \( d \) from a point to a line. However, since these lines are parallel, we can simplify our approach by noting that any point on one line will be equidistant from the other line.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Identify a Point on One Line:**

   Choose a convenient point on the first line \( 3x - 4y + 12 = 0 \). Let's set \( x = 0 \):

   \[
   3(0) - 4y + 12 = 0 \\
   -4y + 12 = 0 \\
   y = 3
   \]

   So, the point is \( (0, 3) \).

2. **Use the Distance Formula:**

   The distance \( d \) from a point \( (x_0, y_0) \) to the line \( Ax + By + C = 0 \) is given by:

   \[
   d = \frac{|Ax_0 + By_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
   \]

3. **Plug in the Values:**

   For the second line \( 3x - 4y + 7 = 0 \), we have:
   
   - \( A = 3 \)
   - \( B = -4 \)
   - \( C = 7 \)
   
   And the point \( (0, 3) \):
   
   - \( x_0 = 0 \)
   - \( y_0 = 3 \)

   Substitute these into the distance formula:

   \[
   d = \frac{|3(0) + (-4)(3) + 7|}{\sqrt{3^2 + (-4)^2}} \\
   d = \frac{|0 - 12 + 7|}{\sqrt{9 + 16}} \\
   d = \frac{|-5|}{\sqrt{25}} \\
   d = \frac{5}{5} \\
   d = 1
   \]

### Final Answer:

The distance between the two parallel lines is:

\[
\boxed{1}
\]

For the given equations:
1. sqrt(3)x + y = 1 has a slope (m₁) of -sqrt(3).

The distance from the point (-4, 5) to the line 5x - 6y + 4 = 0 can be calculated using the formula for the distance from a point to a line.

Step-by-Step Explanation:
1. **Identify the coefficients**: From the line equation Ax + By + C = 0, A is 5, B is -6, and C is 4.
2. **Plug in the coordinates of the point** (-4, 5) into the formula: |A*(-4) + B*(5) + C|.
3. **Calculate the numerator**: |5*(-4) + (-6)*5 + 4| = |-20 - 30 + 4| = |-46| = 46.
4. **Calculate the denominator**: sqrt(A² + B²) = sqrt(5² + (-6)²) = sqrt(25 + 36) = sqrt(61).
5. **Find the distance**: Divide the numerator by the denominator: 46 / sqrt(61).

\[ d = \frac{|a x_0 + b y_0 + c|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}} \]

To find the coordinates of the points on the **x-axis** that lie at a distance of **4 units** from the line \( \frac{x}{3} + \frac{y}{4} = 1 \), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Rewrite the Line Equation in Standard Form
The given equation is:
\[
\frac{x}{3} + \frac{y}{4} = 1
\]
Multiply both sides by \( 12 \) to eliminate the denominators:
\[
4x + 3y = 12
\]
Subtract \( 12 \) from both sides to get it into standard form (\( ax + by + c = 0 \)):
\[
4x + 3y - 12 = 0
\]

### Step 2: Use the Point-to-Line Distance Formula
The formula for the distance \( d \) from a point \( (x_0, y_0) \) to the line \( ax + by + c = 0 \) is:
\[
d = \frac{|a x_0 + b y_0 + c|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}}
\]
Since we're looking for points on the **x-axis**, their coordinates are of the form \( (x, 0) \). Substitute \( y_0 = 0 \):
\[
d = \frac{|4x - 12|}{5}
\]

### Step 3: Set Up the Equation for the Desired Distance
We want this distance to be equal to **4 units**:
\[
\frac{|4x - 12|}{5} = 4
\]
Multiply both sides by \( 5 \):
\[
|4x - 12| = 20
\]

### Step 4: Solve the Absolute Value Equation
The absolute value equation splits into two cases:
1. \( 4x - 12 = 20 \)
2. \( 4x - 12 = -20 \)

**Case 1:**  
\[
4x - 12 = 20 \\
4x = 32 \\
x = 8
\]

**Case 2:**  
\[
4x - 12 = -20 \\
4x = -8 \\
x = -2
\]

### Step 5: Identify the Points
The points on the **x-axis** that satisfy the condition are:
- \( (8, 0) \)
- \( (-2, 0) \)

### Final Answer
\[
\boxed{\{-2,\ 8\}}
\]

Next, I'll rearrange the terms to bring all variables and constants to one side:

To find the distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line \(24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)\), follow these steps:

### Step 1: Simplify the Equation of the Line
Start by expanding and rearranging the given equation:
\[
\begin{align*}
24(x + 12) &= 10(y - 4) \\
24x + 288 &= 10y - 40 \\
24x - 10y + 328 &= 0
\end{align*}
\]
Now, the equation is in the standard form \(Ax + By + C = 0\), where:
- \(A = 24\)
- \(B = -10\)
- \(C = 328\)

### Step 2: Use the Distance Formula
The distance \(d\) from a point \((x_0, y_0)\) to the line \(Ax + By + C = 0\) is given by:
\[
d = \frac{|A x_0 + B y_0 + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}
\]
Substitute the values into the formula:
\[
d = \frac{|24(-2) + (-10)(2) + 328|}{\sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2}}
\]

### Step 3: Calculate the Numerator
Calculate each term inside the absolute value:
\[
\begin{align*}
24(-2) &= -48 \\
-10(2) &= -20 \\
-48 + (-20) + 328 &= 260 \\
|260| &= 260
\end{align*}
\]

### Step 4: Calculate the Denominator
Calculate the square roots:
\[
\sqrt{24^2 + (-10)^2} = \sqrt{576 + 100} = \sqrt{676} = 26
\]

### Step 5: Compute the Distance
Divide the numerator by the denominator to find the distance:
\[
d = \frac{260}{26} = 10
\]

### Final Answer
The distance from the point \((-2, 2)\) to the line \(24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)\) is:

\[
\boxed{10}
\]

To solve the equation \(12x + y - 9y = 18\) (which simplifies to \(12x - 8y = 18\)) for intercept form:

1. **Simplify the original equation**:
   \[
   12x - 8y = 18
   \]

2. **Find the x-intercept** by setting \(y = 0\):
   \[
   12x = 18 \implies x = \frac{18}{12} = \frac{3}{2}
   \]
   So, the x-intercept is \(\left( \frac{3}{2}, 0 \right)\).

3. **Find the y-intercept** by setting \(x = 0\):
   \[
   -8y = 18 \implies y = \frac{-18}{8} = -\frac{9}{4}
   \]
   So, the y-intercept is \(\left( 0, -\frac{9}{4} \right)\).

4. **Write the equation in intercept form** using the intercepts:
   \[
   \frac{x}{\frac{3}{2}} + \frac{y}{-\frac{9}{4}} = 1
   \]
   This can be rewritten as:
   \[
   \frac{x}{\frac{3}{2}} - \frac{y}{\frac{9}{4}} = 1
   \]

Thus, the equation in intercept form is:

\[
\boxed{\dfrac{x}{\frac{3}{2}} - \dfrac{y}{\frac{9}{4}} = 1}
\]

The x-intercept is \(\boxed{\left( \frac{3}{2}, 0 \right)}\) and the y-intercept is \(\boxed{(0, -\frac{9}{4})}\).

\[ \text{Distance} = \frac{|ax₀ + by₀ + c|}{\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}} \]

\[ \text{Distance} = \frac{15\sqrt{17}}{17} \]

The equation of the line equidistant from \(9x + 6y - 7 = 0\) and \(3x + 2y + 6 = 0\) can be found by scaling both equations to have the same coefficients for \(x\) and \(y\). 

1. Scale \(3x + 2y + 6 = 0\) by multiplying through by 3 to get \(9x + 6y + 18 = 0\).
2. Now, the two lines are:
   - \(9x + 6y - 7 = 0\) (Line 1)
   - \(9x + 6y + 18 = 0\) (Scaled Line 2)
3. The equidistant line is found by averaging the constants: \(\frac{-7 + 18}{2} = 5.5\).
4. Thus, the equidistant line in scaled form is \(9x + 6y + 5.5 = 0\), which simplifies to \(18x + 12y + 11 = 0\) when multiplied by 2.
